## Document 004: 10月宏观数据点评：内外需延续分化格局

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月宏观数据点评：内外需延续分化格局.pdf
Report source: 银泰证券
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
宏观研究
宏观点评
内外需延续分化格局
——10月宏观数据点评
事件：2026 年 11 月 16日，国家统计局发布 10 月份国内宏观经济数据。10
月份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，社会消费品零售总额同比增
长 0.4%；1—10 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，房地产
开发投资同比下降 21.6%。
核心观点：
 10 月国内经济内外需分化格局延续，“供强需弱”特征进一步显现。各
口径表现情况看，出口高增与新兴产业快速发展继续对工业生产形成有
力支撑，10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，高技术制造业、装备
制造业增加值分别增长 17.6%和 10.6%；但内需端进一步走弱，社零受
假期错位影响增速再度放缓，10 月同比增长 0.4%、较上月回落 0.7 个百
分点，1—10 月固定资产投资同比下降 8.1%，房地产开发投资下降
21.6%，降幅较上月分别扩大 0.9和 1.0个百分点。

 往后看，伴随各项稳增长政策落地，包括新型政策性金融工具、专项债
等资金加快形成实物工作量，以及房地产因城施策与城市更新持续推进
等，后期国内经济增长动能有望边际改善。但政策力度与节奏、外需持
续性及海外货币政策变化仍是主要不确定性来源。
 风险提示：1、政策进度不及预期；2、出口回落超预期；3、美联储政策
紧缩超预期。
2026 年 11 月 18 日

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
1、外需与新动能继续对工业生产形成支撑
10 月份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长
4.8%，增速较上月的 5.5%回落 0.7 个百分点，低于
市场一致预期的增长5.3%，1—10月累计增长5.3%。
10 月外需与新动能继续对工业生产形成支撑。
以美元计当月国内出口同比增长35.2%，受此带动规
模以上工业企业出口交货值同比名义增长18.6%，外
需对工业生产的拉动作用持续显现。另一方面，10
月份高技术制造业增加值同比增长17.6%，装备制造
业增长 10.6%，分别快于全部规模以上工业 12.8 和
5.8个百分点；3D打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器
人产量同比分别增长85.9%、30.0%和26.0%，新能源
汽车产量增长17.1%，新动能产业继续为工业生产注
入强劲动能。
图表 1 规模以上工业增加值增速（%） 图表 2 规上工业企业出口交货值增速（%）
数据来源：同花顺 iFinD、银泰证券研究所

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
分三大门类看，10 月采矿业增加值同比增长
0.7%，制造业增长 5.4%，电力、热力、燃气及水生
产和供应业增长4.4%，较上月分别放缓0.6、0.6和
1.2个百分点。
分行业看，10 月份 41 个大类行业中有 28 个行
业增加值保持同比增长，增长面为68.3%。其中装备
与电子相关行业景气度整体领先，计算机、通信和其
他电子设备制造业增长19.4%，专用设备制造业增长
11.5%，铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造
业增长9.2%，通用设备制造业增长8.5%，电气机械
和器材制造业增长8.1%，汽车制造业增长 0.9%。传
统周期行业多数继续承压，有色金属冶炼和压延加工
业下降4.4%，煤炭开采和洗选业下降2.9%，非金属
矿物制品业下降 2.3%，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业
下降1.7%，化学原料和化学制品制造业仅增长1.3%。

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
2、假期错位影响社零增速再度放缓
10月份，社会消费品零售总额同比增长0.4%，
增速较上月的1.1%回落0.7个百分点，低于市场一
致预期的0.8%，1—10月累计增长1.0%。当月社零
增速放缓，中秋假期错位带来明显扰动，但国内消费
需求偏弱同样是重要原因。
按消费类型分，10月商品零售额同比增长0.3%
（上月为+1.0%），餐饮增长0.7%（上月为+2.1%），
增速分别放缓0.7 和1.4 个百分点。10月份限额以
上单位消费品零售额同比下降4.9%，降幅较上月扩
大2.8个百分点，自4 月以来持续处于负增长区间。
主要商品类别中，汽车类同比下降21.4%（上月为
-21.2%），继续对社零整体形成拖累；此外通讯器材
类同比增长6.5%（上月为31.1%），增速较上月明显
放缓，家用电器和音像器材类增长2.5%（上月为
1.9%）边际有所改善。其它商品类别中，烟酒类增长
2.5%（上月为13.8%），饮料类增长0.3%（上月为

5.0%），文化办公用品类增长4.1%（上月为7.9%），
中西药品类增长3.7%（上月为5.0%），化妆品类增
长2.8%（上月为5.4%），增速较上月均有不同程度
的放缓。

3、固投延续调整，新动能投资较快增长
1—10月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同
比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1—9 月的-7.2%扩大 0.9个百分

### Page 7

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
点，低于市场一致预期的-6.8。民间固定资产投资同
比下降 11.4%，降幅继续扩大。
分领域看，基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，降幅
较 1—9月扩大 0.9个百分点；制造业投资下降 2.9%，
降幅扩大 0.8个百分点。
结构上看，投资向新动能集中的特征进一步强
化。1—10 月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，占
全部投资比重 16.2%，拉动全部投资增长 1.3个百分
点；设备工器具购置投资增长 8.9%，占比 20.3%、
比上年同期提高 3.2个百分点，拉动全部投资增长 1.5
个百分点。高技术产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信
息服务业投资增长 20.5%、航空、航天器及设备制造
业增长 10.2%、电子及通信设备制造业增长 6.6%。
基建投资落地进程偏慢，1—10月电力、热力、
燃气及水生产和供应业投资下降 7.7%（1—9 月为
-6.8%），交通运输、仓储和邮政业增长 0.7%（1—9
月为+1.9%），水利、环境和公共设施管理业下降

14.4%（1—9月为-12.8%），投资增速较上月均有不
同程度的放缓。

4、房地产延续筑底，开发投资降幅扩大
1—10 月份，全国房地产开发投资同比下降
21.6%，降幅较1—9 月的-20.6%扩大1.0个百分点。
房屋施工面积下降13.0%，新开工面积下降24.4%，
竣工面积下降 26.6%，开发端各环节仍全面处于低

### Page 9

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
位。
销售端看，1—10 月新建商品房销售面积同比
下降13.7%，降幅较1—9月扩大 0.5个百分点；销
售额下降14.3%，降幅扩大0.4个百分点。
相对积极的是，二手房交易持续改善。1—10
月全国二手房交易网签面积 67971 万平方米、同比
增长11.4%，增速较1—9月加快 0.7个百分点。
房价方面，10月份一线城市新建商品住宅销售
价格环比由上月上涨0.1%转为持平，其中上海、深
圳分别上涨0.2%和0.1%，广州持平、北京下降0.1%；
二线城市环比由持平转为下降0.1%，三线城市下降
0.2%、降幅与上月相同；70个大中城市中新建商品
住宅价格环比上涨城市14个，比上月减少3 个。
二手住宅方面，一线城市环比上涨0.2%、涨幅
与上月持平（上海+0.5%、广州+0.2%、深圳+0.1%、
北京持平），二线、三线城市均下降0.3%；二手住
宅价格环比上涨城市7 个，比上月减少2 个。
同比看，一二三线城市新建商品住宅价格降幅

分别收窄0.4、0.4和0.3个百分点，二手住宅价格
降幅分别收窄1.1、0.4和0.4个百分点，一线城市
价格企稳趋势继续得到确认，但全国层面地产仍处
于筑底阶段。

公司簡介 
中砂為先進製程鑽石碟第一把交椅，早期業
務專注於傳統砂輪，後取得鑽石碟技術專利
授權，並因應晶圓代工廠客戶在地化策略，
切入半導體領域。中砂主要有三大事業部，
砂輪事業部、鑽石事業部、晶圓事業部。
3Q26 營收組成為 ABU(含砂輪子公司如鴻
記、KTC、MKS、MGT) 23%、DBU 32%、
SBU 45%。

主要客戶：台積電、美光、聯電、意法半導
體、英特爾，以及載板廠欣興等 
主要競爭對手：

王彥鈞 stanley.wang@sinopac.com

中砂 (1560 TT) 
以砂為鏡，可以升良率

永豐觀點 
以銅為鏡，可以正衣冠；以砂為鏡，可以升良率。 
 投資評價與建議 
目標價調升至 1,000 元：(1) 3Q26 ABU 獲利改善優於預期，帶動公司平均毛利率
升至 42.2%(+2.2pts QoQ)且具延續力，(2) 產能建置加速，推動 2027 年獲利提
升，(3) 購置三峽廠，打開 DBU 擴產瓶頸。目標價隨 2028 年 EPS 調整，由 860
元(33x 2028 EPSF)上修至 1,000 元(32x 2028 EPSF)。

ESG 評析 
中砂企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

### Page 2

2

中砂為先進製程鑽石碟第一把交椅：中砂成立於 1953 年，2005 年上市，目前資
本額約 14.6 億元，早期業務專注於傳統砂輪，後取得鑽石碟技術專利授權，並因
應晶圓代工廠客戶在地化策略，切入半導體領域。中砂主要有三大事業部，砂輪事
業部、鑽石事業部、晶圓事業部。3Q26 營收組成為 ABU(含砂輪子公司如鴻記、
KTC、MKS、MGT) 23%、DBU 32%、SBU 45%。中砂總部位於新北市鶯歌區，
砂輪、鑽石相關產品主要在鶯歌、樹林、湖口廠生產，而再生/測試晶圓主要在新
竹竹北、竹南廠生產，另在日本有 1 座廠房、泰國有 2 座廠房，另已在三峽新採
購 1 座廠房。客戶涵蓋半導體/記憶體製造商如台積電、美光、聯電、意法半導體、
英特爾，以及載板廠欣興等。 
3Q26 毛利率優於預期：中砂第三季稅後 EPS 3.91 元(+12.8%QoQ，+79.8%YoY)，
優於我們預期的 3.78 元，主因毛利率 42.2%季增約 2.2 個百分點高於預期，係來
自(1) 鑽石事業部營收占比及自身毛利率改善，係受到客戶惠先進製程出貨比重增
加，帶動高接產品放大，(2) ABU 產品組合改善，來自鑽石砂輪營收占比由 40%提
升至約 50%。 
加速擴產並改善獲利：我們認為 4Q26 的毛利率仍有提升空間，係考量(1) 先進製
程及 HBM 對鑽石碟產品用量增加，且公司產能由 3Q26 6.5 萬顆/月提升至 4Q26 
8 萬顆/月(原規劃 7 萬顆/月)，2nm/A14 用鑽石碟出貨量的增加將帶動 DBU 佔比
提升及獲利改善，(2) 部分測試晶圓及再生晶圓產品自 4Q26 起對所有客戶漲價約
10%，加上測試晶圓比重回落，可改善獲利率，(3) ABU 7 月漲價的效果可在 4Q26
顯現，加上鑽石砂輪、晶圓倒角砂輪、鑽石鍍膜等高階工具比重提升。另外，研究
部認為 2027 年中砂獲利成長動能仍將來自 DBU 的成長，目前公司規劃將鑽石碟
產能提升到明年底的 11 萬顆/月(+37.5%YoY)，以因應記憶體和美系 IDM 廠的擴
大採用、成熟製程客戶的產能擴增、以及晶圓代工廠先進製程積極擴產，同時預期
公司將適時與客戶們共同分擔生產成本的增加；SBU 則透過改造部分產線，並增
加表面缺陷檢測設備，來滿足客戶 HBM 對於特殊晶圓的需求。 
下一站，半導體用砂輪：由於既有樹林廠難以滿足客戶對鑽石碟中期需求，中砂於
今日董事會通過投入不超過 5.4 億新台幣於三峽購置廠房，用於擴增鑽石碟及半導
體廠用的鑽石砂輪產能，因應 CoWoS/SoIC/HBM 等先進封裝和晶背供電應用，
我們認為有望於 2028 年起貢獻營收並改善 DBU 產品組合，並隨 HPC 產品(及搭
配的記憶體)封裝規格提升和製程轉進而成長。 
再次上修預估：研究部預估中砂 2026 年全年營收 103.2 億(+26.6%YoY)，毛利率
40.4%，稅後淨利 21.9 億(+61.0%YoY)，EPS 14.60 元(原估 14.47 元)，獲利上調
主因 ABU 獲利率改善速度優於預期。2027 年營收 133.6 億(+29.5%YoY)，毛利
率 45.4%，稅後淨利 33.2 億(+51.5%YoY)，EPS 22.08 元(原估 20.26 元)。2028
年營收 174.2 億(+30.4%YoY)，毛利率 47.8%，稅後淨利 47.6 億(+43.3%YoY)，
EPS 31.65 元(原估 26.05 元)。

## Document 046: 20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。隨著市場風險偏好下降，避險資金轉向美國公債，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於 10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準，法股單日下跌約 1.5%，歐元同步走弱。市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇，但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變。隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升，具技術優勢、財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利，而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落，仍保持穩健成長；就業重返擴張區間，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性。惟價格指數續升創高，出口與進口動能轉弱，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月增加 13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口 4,208 億美元，分別月增 1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主。累計前 8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元，較去年同期縮減 19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

灣 
出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月消費者物價指數年增 2.73%。 ⚫ 9 月出口額為 872.2 億美元。 
重要
事件 
⚫ 美國國際貿易委員會（USITC)以 337 條款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關
產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達、鴻海及旗下相關企業等遭
列名，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。 
短評 
⚫ 9 月出口續創佳績，反映 AI 相關需求及科技產品拉貨動能強勁，外需
表現維持熱絡；9 月 CPI 創近兩年半新高，主要受天候因素及能源價格
影響，但主計總處預期 10 月將回落。

一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由 56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月修正後 928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計 2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期，帶動企業投資與生產力提升；

用相關內容。

5

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗，改口表示「仍有可能」採取軍事行動，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場。調查對象涵蓋全球約 20 家企業，包括台灣的鴻海、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

則表示，337 調查屬企業間智慧財產權爭議，政府將持續關注案情發展，並於必
要時提供業者相關協助；若最終認定違反 337 條款，涉案產品可能面臨輸美限制，
對 AI 伺服器供應鏈帶來潛在影響。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修，並且在 8 月陸續復工後，帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致；此外，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增 0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%，主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動，有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響，民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫，預期通膨率高於 2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來，近期歐中談判達成 16 項共識，未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI）相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將 2026 年東亞與太平洋地區（EAP）經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應，也更仰賴補貼，此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力，運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求，持續性的補貼可能難以為繼。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長 23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型，經濟前景仍
偏正向。

用相關內容。

9

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在 AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會（USITC）於 10 月 9 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

## Document 101: 2026年7～9月期のGDP（1次速報）結果

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7～9月期のGDP（1次速報）結果.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 / 2

2026 年 11 月 17 日

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）結果

～個人消費が力強さを欠き、4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表した 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比－0.0%（年率換算   
－0.1%）と減少した。設備投資が前期比＋1.6%と高めの伸びとなった一方、個人消費が同－0.3%と 2 四半期連
続で減少したことなどが下押し要因となり、小幅ながら 4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。なお、輸入が鉱物
性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は－0.6%ポイントと大きめのマイナスとなったが、公的在庫の寄与
度が＋0.5%ポイントとなり、おおむね相殺された。 
過去 3 四半期にわたって潜在成長率を上回る高めの伸びが続いていたことや、設備投資が堅調に増加したこ
とを踏まえると、今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の動きが途切れたとは判断できないが、景気が力強さを欠
いていることも事実だろう。特に GDP の過半を占める個人消費に弱さがみられたことは懸念材料である。今後は、
良好な雇用・所得環境に支えられて個人消費は持ち直しへ向かうと期待されるが、消費者物価の伸び拡大や住
宅ローン金利の上昇による家計負担の増加を通じて、消費の減少が続くリスクには警戒が必要である。 
なお、物価上昇分を含んだ名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.4%（年率換算＋1.6%）と、10 四半期連続のプラス成長
となった。物価上昇圧力は依然として強く、コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比  
＋2.4%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.4%）と、高い伸びが続いた。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度（%ポイント）

### Page 2

 1 次速報値を踏まえた、2026 年 7～9 月期における需要項目別の動向（実質ベース）は以下の通り。

① 実質個人消費（前期比－0.3%） 
 家計を取り巻く所得環境は改善した（実質雇用者報酬は前期比＋0.3%）が、前期に自動車や
エアコンが一時的な要因によって増加した反動で耐久財が前期比－3.5%と減少した上、夏場
の地震や台風、長雨などの自然災害・天候不順によって消費の機会に恵まれなかったこと、食
料品の値上げや住宅ローン金利の上昇など今後の生活不安につながる話題も多く節約志向が
強まったことから、半耐久財や非耐久財も減少した。ただし、サービスは 9 月のシルバーウイー
クの日並びが良かったこともあり、小幅に増加した。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－1.1%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数が 2 四半期ぶりに減少する中、建築コストの増加も逆風とな
り、住宅投資は 2 四半期連続で減少した。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比＋1.6%） 
 企業の省力化や生産性向上に向けた投資意欲は強く、特に 7～9 月期はサーバーや半導体製
造装置といった AI 関連投資も好調だったことで、設備投資は 3 四半期ぶりに増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度＋0.0%ポイント） 
 前期に大きめのプラス寄与となったこともあり、民間在庫の寄与は横ばいとなった。 
 なお、公的在庫は国家備蓄原油の減少幅が縮小したことから、前期比寄与度＋0.5%ポイントと
なった。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費（前期比－0.0%） 
 前期に高めの伸びとなったこともあり、横ばいだった。 
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－1.1%） 
 名目では増加したものの、工事コストの増加を受けたデフレーターの上昇に追いついておらず、
実質では 2 四半期連続で減少した。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度－0.6%ポイント、実質輸出：前期比－0.2%、実質輸入：同＋2.5%） 
 輸出は前期比－0.2%と減少した。財は堅調な米国向けや中東向けの持ち直しを受けて増加し
た一方、サービスは前期に研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れた反動で減少した。 
 輸入は前期比＋2.5%と増加した。特に財が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直した。ただし、サービス
については減少した。 
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は－0.6%ポイントと大きめのマイナスになった。

We hosted Kinsus’ 3Q26 post-result follow-up call today (Nov 
10th). Key takeaways include: 
(1) Kinsus mentioned it is getting more market share from existing 
server CPU customers, AI GPU customer, and ASIC customers. 
Despite not having much market share gain opportunity in its AI 
server CPU products, the company mentioned the volume growth 
could still be 150% YoY in AI server CPU shipment in 2027 vs. 2026, 
suggesting a good revenue/GM growth outlook going into 2027/28.  
(2) Kinsus guides 2027/28 revenue to go up by at least 30% YoY 
each year while GM should be at least 35/40% in 2027/28, 
considering the orders/capacity/UTR/product mix differences, 
despite the key upside risk will be on like for like base pricing hike 
(the guidance above does not include any pricing hike assumption, 
but this could lift the revenue growth by another 15ppt in both 
years although not guaranteed, per the company). 
(3) For 4Q26, the company guided revenue to go up by 10% QoQ, 
with GM being expanded into 35% (from ~34% in 3Q26), despite a 
much more favorable than 3Q product mix and ~5-10% pricing hike 
on ABF & BT substrates. For 1Q27, the company expects some 
seasonal issues on consumer electronics and contact lens, and 
believes revenue should be flat to slightly up QoQ, despite possible 
GM slowdown during the low season. 
(4) In terms of the future investment plan, Kinsus mentioned it will 
only need to invest in the land/infrastructure/construction etc. while 
end customers will take care of the equipment; normally, equipment 
accounted for 60% of the total CAPEX while the rest only accounts 
for 40% of the total investment.  
(5) Kinsus mentioned it now holds ~70% of the AI server CPU 
substrate market share, and the company believes its market share 
should be pretty stable in this product cycle. For AI GPU, Kinsus

mentioned it expects its share to increase from 10% now to 30% in 2027 for its existing
customer’s supply chain. Kinsus also mentioned it is working on several ASIC customers’
substrates product, which could also be the key revenue/profit driver for 2027 and
beyond.
(6) In terms of the order forecast, the company mentioned it has not yet seen any order
cut or order delay on its ABF substrates business, but the company continues to
maintain its cautious but positive view on the overall substrate industry outlook.

Investment view
We are positive on Kinsus’ 3Q result (GM expanded by 7.8ppt QoQ in 3Q - see here), and
believe the company’s GM expansion trend will continue in coming quarters, despite the
company only guiding 1ppt QoQ growth on GM in 4Q (however, the company is also
giving a conservative guidance on profitability. Kinsus only guided GM to be 27-28% in
3Q26, but it ended up with 33.9% in its 3Q result). Moreover, we believe Kinsus’
proactive capacity expansion plan (1 new plant every year from 2025 to 2029) will start

to help the company to outperform peers from 2Q27 (the new K6-B plant starts
contributing revenue from 2Q27) which should also help the company to enjoy better
profitability in coming quarters, as the new plants will focus on high-end projects, whose
GM is generally 10ppt+ higher than the company’s average level).
Overall, we maintain our Buy rating on Kinsus, and we expect the better market share in
AI server CPU and its better position in the high-end ABF substrate industry to

strengthen into the long term.
We maintain our Buy rating with an unchanged 12m TP of NT$1,515, based on the
same 19.5x 2028E EPS, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average
P/E (14.2x).
Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate

shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end
ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe

Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by a better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the
unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

contribution would represent a single-digit percentage of EMC’s total revenue.

boards. Our positive view reflects EMC’s technological capabilities and our
preference for market share leaders. While supplier diversification can strengthen
long-term supply security, we believe the ability to sustain yields and deliver
consistent product quality over time remains a key consideration in supplier

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 174: 261112_daiwa_eink

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_daiwa_eink.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: E Ink held an investor call today, 12 November, after 3Q26 results were 
announced on 6 November (see our memo). Overall, its 3Q26 gross margin of 52.2% (vs. 
58.7% in 2Q26 and 56.0% in 3Q25) missed our estimate of 59.0% and the consensus 
estimate of 58.2%, mainly due to less revenue scale and unfavourable product mix within 
consumer electronics segment (ie, more module, less film). E Ink also talked about their 
cooperation with Taiflex (8039 TT, not rated) on PTFE material and management is 
optimistic towards the development progress.

We have a Hold (3) rating on E Ink with a 12-month TP of TWD165, based on a target PER 
of 14x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. For more information on the company, 
please refer to our latest flash, Downgrading: weaker-than-expected order visibility, on 13 
August 2026.

What’s the impact  
• 3Q26 results recap. E Ink’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD9,936m (-2.7% QoQ, -4.6% YoY) 
was roughly in line with our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates. Its 3Q26 gross 
margin of 52.2% (vs. 58.7% in 2Q26 and 56.0% in 3Q25) missed our estimate of 
59.0% and the consensus estimate of 58.2%, mainly due to less revenue scale and 
unfavourable product mix within its consumer electronics segment (ie, more module, 
less film). Its 3Q26 net profit of TWD2,652m (-29.0% QoQ, -37.3% YoY) beat our and 
consensus estimates by 21.6% and 15.2%, respectively. Its 3Q26 EPS came in at 
TWD2.29. Note E Ink’s 3Q26 product mix was: consumer electronics 54.6% (-9.4% 
YoY), IoT (including ESL and large signage) 45.4% (+1.8% YoY). 
• 2026 and 2027 guidance. E Ink slightly revised down its 2026 full-year guidance: total 
revenue to grow by 9% YoY (down from previous 10-15%); consumer electronics to 
decline by 15% YoY, negatively affected by the memory price hikes; IoT to grow by 
35%+ YoY; and signage to account for high-single-digit % of total revenue. For 2027, 
the company is still positive on the growth of the IoT segment, but consumer the 
consumer electronics segment will depend on how the new generations of end 
products perform. 
• Cooperation with Taiflex (8039 TT, not rated) on PTFE. E Ink has announced it will 
work with Taiflex to co-develop the next generation of CCL material – PTFE. The two 
companies will combine Taiflex’s expertise in CCL and high-end electronics materials, 
and E Ink’s capabilities in mass production, surface treatment and roll-to-roll 
processing. E Ink expects to see preliminary results by 2027 and significant revenue 
contribution from 2028. 
• Capacity expansion and future trends. E Ink’s H5 plant is performing on track and 
its utilisation rate and yield rate are improving. The H6 plant has also started to install 
equipment in November 2026, and is scheduled for a trial run in 2027 and contribution 
to revenue in 2028. The company expects both plants to have capability to 
manufacture large signage. Combining the trend that E Ink’s customers and the 
retailers are now planning to implement more larger signages (mid-sized, 10-15 
inches, vs. 2 inches for ESL) as retail advertisement, the company is confident that 
signage will replace ESL to become the fastest growing segment.

Country

What we recommend 
We have a Hold (3) rating on E Ink with a 12-month TP of TWD165, based on a target PER 
of 14x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Based on our 2026/27E EPS, the stock 
is currently trading at PERs of 12.3x/11.3x, vs. its past-3-year trading range of 11-32x. Key 
upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected ESL demand, 2) stronger-
/weaker-than-expected eReader sales on macroeconomic headwinds, and 3) progress on 
capacity expansion being early/delayed.

For Pegatron, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$514bn (+32% QoQ, +61% YoY), as 
we expect to see more contribution from AI server going into 4Q26. We expect GM to 
slightly decrease to 4.2% (-0.3ppt QoQ and +0.1%ppt YoY). We continue to see 4Q26

continued peak season for its communication business. 
For Compal, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$282bn (+16% QoQ, +48% YoY) with 
increasing contribution from AI server  as the company continue to expand from 
motherboard (L6) to system level (L10/L11). We expect GM to slightly increase to 4.6% 
(+0.2ppt QoQ and -1.2ppt YoY) as the YoY contraction is due to shift in product mix (we 
expect PC business to account for 60% in 4Q26 vs. 71% in 4Q25).  
For Asus, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$283bn (+2% QoQ, +40% YoY), with solid 
growth from its server business, offsetting the weakness from its PC business, although 
we continue to expect a more premium PC product mix. We expect GM to decrease to 
14.8% (-1.6ppt QoQ and -1.1ppt YoY) due to the change in product mix.

Earnings revisions 
Quanta 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 3% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
up our 2026E revenue estimates by 2%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 7%/1% to reflect a product mix with higher contribution from the server business (we 
revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 1/2%). We now expect the GM to contract 
going forward due to the higher server business contribution, thus revise down GPM by 
0.3/0.2/0.1ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Pegatron 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 20% to factor in 3Q26 results (we 
maintain our 2026E revenue estimates). We revise down 2027 earnings estimates by 5% 
to reflect a higher % for minority interest while revise up 2028 earnings estimates by 3% 
with improved GM (we revise down our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 9/6% to reflect 
a more conservative assumption for its communication business). We now expect the 
GM to remain stable going forward, thus revise up GPM by 0.4/0.7/0.5ppt in 
2026/27/28E.

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Quanta 
Quanta is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, tablets, 
consumer electronics, servers, etc. We are Neutral rated on Quanta. We expect the 
company’s earnings to grow in 2026-28E, driven by 1) growing AI server demand from 
Quanta’s CSP customers, and 2) general server demand recovery. However, we see P/E 
valuation as fair at the current level, given the positives appear to have been mostly 
factored in the share price and the current upside to our target price is relatively lower 
compared to its peers under coverage. We would consider becoming more positive if the 
AI server demand from its CSP clients turn out to be above our expectations.

### Page 7

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$280 is based on a 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E.  
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers. 
Pegatron is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of 
smartphones, PCs, consumer electronics, and servers, etc. While we like Pegatron’s 
product line diversification into servers, AI servers, and automotive electronics, we are 
Sell rated as we see revenue contribution from AI server remaining small in near-term, 
and our 2027E target P/E is near the company’s current trading P/E. We expect 
smartphone business will remain the major revenue contributor from 2026E to 2028E, 
and we see the AI server related revenue contribution remains 4% in 2026E (vs. peers 
14%~87%). Catalysts for further upside will be its faster-than-expected progress in AI 
servers. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Pegatron 
We are Sell rated on Pegatron with a 12-month target price of NT$71. Our TP is based 
on a 9.7x 2027E P/E, which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E. 
Key upside risks: 1) stronger-than-expected smartphone market recovery, 2) 
faster-than-expected AI server ramp-up, and 3) stronger-than-expected demand on 
consumer electronics.

Investment thesis- Compal 
Compal is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, tablets, 
consumer electronics, servers, etc. While we like Compal’s solid position in the PC ODM 
market and its expansion into AI servers, we are Neutral rated as we see the contribution 
from AI servers as limited in the near-term given its limited exposure to cloud server 
customers, who are the early adopters of AI servers. Meanwhile, we believe valuation 
already reflects near-term positives. We model Compal earnings growth in 2026-26E to 
be mainly driven by commercial PC demand recovery and server business ramping up. 
We see P/E valuation as fair at current levels compared to peers given its mild earnings 
growth in 2026-28E.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Compal 
We are Neutral-rated on Compal with a 12-month target price of NT$45. Our target 
price is based on 14.7x 2027E P/E, which is +0.5 STDV above the company’s 2018-23 
average P/E. 
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger/weaker-than-expected demand for tablets.

### Page 8

Investment thesis- Asus 
Asus is a multinational computer hardware and electronics company based in Taiwan 
and is the top motherboard and gaming brand globally and also a top-three consumer 
notebook vendor, per the company. We are Neutral rated on Asus considering (1) 
challenging PC market under end of Win10 replacement cycle, rising memory costs, and 
consumption pull in in 1H25 under China government subsidies and tariff concerns, (2) 
ASUS brand AI servers remain small in shipment. The stock currently trades at 14x 2027E 
PE, close to our target PE multiple (14.8x), which we think is fair.  
We would turn more positive on ASUS if we see (1) its PC revenue growth stronger than 
expected which could be driven by faster than expected product mix upgrade toward AI 
PC or gaming PC, or (2) faster than expected AI servers shipment ramp up, which could 
be driven by penetration in leading enterprises or CSPs (cloud services providers).

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Asus 
We are Neutral-rated on ASUS with a 12-month target price of NT$1,000. Our target 
price is based on 14.8x 2027E P/E, which is +0.5 STDV above the company’s 2018-23 
average P/E 
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger / weaker-than-expected PC market 
growth; 2) faster / slower-than-expected AI and gaming PC growth; and 3) faster / 
slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

## Document 266: [005380] 현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대4분기 반등 기대 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대4분기 반등 기대 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

목표주가 (하향) 520,000 원 
현재주가 295,500 원 
상승여력 76.0 %

3Q26 Review: 악재의 중첩, 
4분기까지 지켜보자 
최정환 CFA

jeonghwan.choi@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 1,640,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
739,000 
방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  November 2026, No.308

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

1 
Topics  Regulatory reform in India's insurance sector

Over the past quarter century, the Indian government has pursued the liberalization of the country's insurance market, 
with the aim of bringing stability to people's living standards and strengthening financial intermediation functions. 
Recently, however, it has begun to reverse course, tightening rules in order to better protect consumers.

■ India's insurance market has grown rapidly with deregulation 
The Indian government has long pursued 
the development of the country's insurance 
market, with the aim of bringing stability to 
people's living standards and strengthening 
financial intermediation functions. This 
stance can be attributed to two background 
factors:  
The first is that India's social security 
system remains underdeveloped. As a result, 
enrollment in health and life insurance can 
provide a vital safety net for mitigating the 
risk of sharp declines in household income 
due to unexpected illnesses or accidents.  
The second is that the sustainable 
development of the Indian economy requires 
the accumulation of long-term and stable 
domestic capital, separate from short-term 
bank deposits, in order to finance the 
development of infrastructure and the 
nurturing of industry. The hope has been that 
the enormous sums of money collected and 
managed by insurance companies can serve 
as a reliable source for this long-term 
financing.  
These factors have led the Indian 
government to walk a path of gradual 
deregulation over the past quarter century. 
The process began with the liberalization of 
market entry by private and foreign 
companies in 2000, which was followed by 
a lifting of the ban on "bancassurance" (the 
sale of insurance through bank branches) in 
2002, the raising of the ceiling on foreign 
ownership, and other reforms.  
This series of deregulatory measures 
spurred the entry of numerous private and foreign 
firms, causing market competition to intensify. Several 
Japanese insurers, including Nippon Life and the Tokio 
Marine Group, also entered the market, helping to drive 
investment in India.  
Besides deregulation related to market entry and 
sales, other factors also contributed to the rapid growth 
of India's insurance market, namely the expansion of 
the middle class, improvements in household financial 
literacy, and the digitalization of insurance services. 
Premium income grew at a pace exceeding economic 
growth, and in recent years insurance companies' assets 
under management have reached a level equivalent to 
Year Event
Establishment of the IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory
and Development Authority of India)
Acceptance of market entry applications from
domestic private firms and foreign entities
(foreign equity cap set at 26%)
2002

through banks or bank-owned distribution

incentives to sell products and inappropriate sales practices. This should shore up the credibility of the 
insurance market and the foundation for its sustainable development. 
On the minus side, there are worries about side effects arising from the transition. As rules on selling 
become stricter, insurance companies and the financial institutions acting as insurance agents will inevitably 
see some temporary moderation in the pace of their earnings growth. Moreover, given that insurance 
companies and financial institutions are major investors in India's financial markets, tighter regulation is 
likely to exert downward pressure on equity and bond prices. 
These changes in the insurance market provide an important perspective for assessing the future 
trajectory of the Indian economy. They illustrate that rapid market expansion driven by liberalization can 
create a variety of problems. And this dynamic is not limited to the insurance sector, as it can also occur 
across many other industries. At the same time, the redesign and recalibration of regulations in response to 
such developments can provide a foundation for a new stage of growth. The new insurance regulations 
could constitute an early example of this phenomenon, so will be closely watched to see what changes they 
bring in the years ahead.  
  (Shotaro Kumagai)

Topics  Talent travel curbs may weigh on China’s growth

In September, the Chinese government introduced new exit-entry rules. Indicative of an effort to retain talent in order 
to prevent technology leakage, the rules could narrow opportunities for China to absorb overseas knowledge and weigh 
on its economic growth over the medium to long term.

■ Scope of overseas travel restrictions expanded 
On September 15, the Chinese 
government brought in new rules 
specifying conditions and screening 
procedures for entry and exit. The new 
rules explicitly state that the Ministry of 
Commerce or other departments may 
prohibit Chinese citizens from leaving the 
country if they violate export-control or 
technology import/export regulations and 
are deemed likely to threaten national 
industrial or technological security.  
Under China's Exit and Entry 
Administration Law, which came into 
effect in 2013, defendants and suspects in 
criminal cases, as well as persons involved 
in unsettled civil cases whom courts have 
prohibited from leaving the country, had 
already been subject to exit restrictions. In 
addition, Chinese citizens could be denied 
permission to leave the country if the 
competent departments determined that 
their departure might endanger national 
security or interests. Aside from formal exit 
bans, personnel who handle state secrets 
and officials in the public sector have long 
been subject to controls such as centralized 
passport management and the need to 
obtain approval in advance of overseas travel. What the new rules effectively do is make it clear that exit 
can also be restricted when there are issues from the standpoint of economic security. 
Against this backdrop, it was reported at the end of September that overseas travel restrictions already 
imposed on key personnel in the AI and semiconductor sectors had been expanded to include their spouses, 
children, and other family members. According to these reports, these key personnel include prominent AI 
startup founders, senior executives at major private enterprises, and core R&D talent. Although the measure 
does not constitute a blanket travel ban, affected individuals and their families are reportedly required to 
obtain government approval for exit even in the case of short private trips or business travel. 
This regulatory tightening is thought to be related to the technological rivalry between the U.S. and China. 
As competition surrounding critical technologies such as generative AI intensifies, the Chinese government 
appears to view not only technologies themselves, but also the researchers and engineers who create them, 
as strategic assets. By widening travel curbs to encompass family members, the authorities are likely 
seeking to prevent overseas emigration by individuals or the relocation of R&D operations abroad. A 
noteworthy aspect of the recent reports is that the controls apparently now extend beyond the public sector 
to key personnel at private enterprises, as well as their families. Restrictions like these on talent mobility 
could undermine the mechanisms of technology absorption and productivity improvement that have 
hitherto facilitated China's growth.

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  November 2026, No.308

5 
■ China's productivity gains have been supported by market opening and technology 
absorption 
Productivity exerts a substantial influence on 
economic growth. Total factor productivity (TFP), an 
indicator of an economy's overall production 
efficiency, provides an estimate of efficiency in 
production that cannot be explained solely in terms of 
inputs of capital and labor, and reflects factors such as 
levels of technology and efficiency of resource 
allocation. 
China's TFP has been rising for many years. In 
addition to the transition to a market economy and the 
growth of private enterprises since the country adopted 
the policy of reform and opening up, Chinese 
productivity has benefitted from the absorption of 
foreign technology and managerial know‑how through 
trade and direct investment. A labor shift from 
agriculture to manufacturing and other sectors, along 
with competition among firms and the weeding out of 
weaker players, has also helped deliver productivity 
gains across the economy.  
Nevertheless, China's TFP remains low relative to 
other major economies. According to estimates from 
Penn World Table, China's TFP in 2023 was less than 
half that of the U.S., and it was also below the levels not 
only of Germany and other major European economies 
and of South Korea and Japan, but of Thailand and 
Malaysia, too. Factors behind this include inefficient 
resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced 
technologies and management practices. Preferential 
financial and institutional treatment for state‑owned 
enterprises, regional protectionism, and mechanisms 
that impede market entry and exit have distorted the 
allocation of resources, preventing capital and labor 
from being fully utilized by more productive firms and sectors. Moreover, although China has achieved 
strong competitiveness in certain advanced industries, such as electric vehicles and semiconductors, the 
leading-edge technologies and management practices developed in these sectors have not sufficiently 
diffused to the service sector and small and medium‑sized enterprises, which form the foundation of the 
economy, and this has constrained overall productivity growth.  
■ Moves to contain technology could hinder growth over the medium to long term 
Efforts to retain talent in order to prevent technology leakage could further delay China's productivity 
catch‑up with advanced economies. While restrictions on overseas travel by elite professionals may help 
with the retention of domestic R&D talent and protection of current technological advantages, they could 
also reduce opportunities to absorb overseas knowledge and to jointly develop new technologies with 
foreign partners. The transfer of technology and know‑how does not occur solely through the sharing of 
"explicit knowledge," in the form of academic papers, technical blueprints, and so on. "Tacit knowledge," 
which is transmitted through face‑to‑face discussion, collaborative work, and the acquisition of experience 
in production settings, is also crucial. If travel controls delay or prevent participation in international 
conferences, joint research projects, and technology exchanges with overseas companies, opportunities to 
absorb such knowledge may be lost, slowing technological innovation and improvements in production 
processes. Furthermore, if obtaining travel approval becomes more time‑consuming or less predictable, the 
overseas assignment or international deployment of technical personnel could also be obstructed. 
The new rules also cover the entry of foreign nationals. If uncertainty surrounding the administration of 
entry and exit controls increases, it could create obstacles to attracting and retaining highly skilled talent 
from abroad. At a time when inefficient resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced knowledge 
are already weighing on productivity growth in China, curbs on people-to- people exchange could further

impede both the absorption and creation of knowledge, as well as its practical application. Although the 
magnitude of the impact will depend on the scope of application and actual implementation on the ground, 
restrictions on talent exchanges and overseas travel that reflect excessive emphasis on technology 
protection could slow China's TFP growth over the medium to long term and act as a headwind to the 
Chinese economy by lowering its potential growth rate. 
(Hiromu Komiya)

## Document 373: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-19).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

## Document 381: Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 30
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Macro Outlook 2027: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

We entered 2026 expecting sturdy global growth and slower inflation. The war in Iran 
derailed this inflation forecast, but the global economy proved more resilient than 
feared at the start of the war. 
Even though global headline inflation is on course to end 2026 at 3.7% on a Q4/Q4 
basis—1.3pp higher than our forecast from last year—we estimate that the global 
economy still grew at a remarkably unremarkable 2.7%, in line with potential and only 
0.1pp below our projections from our 2026 outlook (Exhibit 1). Our interpretation is that 
surging AI capex, easier financial conditions, and (in the US, Germany, and parts of Asia) 
fiscal expansion mostly offset the energy supply shock. As a result of these tailwinds, 
GDP growth only modestly underperformed in the US (-0.3pp), Euro area (-0.3pp), and 
China (-0.2pp) while significantly outperforming in Asian economies such as Taiwan 
(+8.6pp) and Korea (+1.9pp) that benefited from surging AI exports.

Growth Remains Solid but Unremarkable in 2027 
We expect another year of solid but unremarkable GDP growth in 2027, but several of 
the impulses that shaped global growth in 2026 will look different. 
The headwind from the energy supply shock will likely flip next year as energy prices 
normalize. Our commodity strategists’ baseline is that continued Middle East supply 
adaptation will lower Brent prices to $78/bbl by end-2027 with a similar normalization 
in global LNG prices (Exhibit 2). Based on these projections, we forecast that the energy 
price impulse to global growth will swing from a 0.4pp drag on global growth in 2026 to 
a modest boost in 2027 (although we see a continued drag in Europe from delayed 
energy cost pass-through to consumers).

Exhibit 1: Inflation Surged, but Growth Proved Resilient in 2026

### Page 3

A positive growth impulse from energy prices should outweigh the effect of tighter 
financial conditions and a smaller fiscal impulse in 2027. Our GS Financial Conditions 
Index (GS FCI) has tightened by around 50bp in the US and by 30bp in developed 
markets (DMs) over the last three months on the back of lower equity prices and higher 
rates. It now implies a moderate drag on growth over the next few quarters (Exhibit 3). 
We do not expect meaningful fiscal consolidation owing to higher rates in 2027, but the 
large fiscal expansions of the last few years are fading. The main exception is Japan, 
where consumption tax cuts, an increase in defense spending, and the Takaichi 
administration’s investment plans point to a meaningful 0.7pp boost to GDP growth. We 
also expect modestly positive impulses in Germany and the US—where the Democratic 
victory in November’s US election suggests some of the spending cuts from the 2025 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act will be reversed—but a continued fiscal drag in the UK that 
leaves the DM fiscal impulse only slightly positive.

Exhibit 2: Oil Prices Are On Course to Partially Normalize, Which Will Turn a Large Headwind into a Modest Tailwind

### Page 4

The main global growth impulse that we do not expect to change in 2027 is increased AI 
capex. We forecast that nominal global AI investment will rise from $1.1tn in 2026 to 
$1.8tn in 2027, mostly driven by an increase of over $500bn in hardware spending in the 
US (Exhibit 4). We expect the investment surge to be frontloaded, with the 
year-over-year change in investment as a share of GDP peaking in 2027H1 (the level of 
investment as a share of GDP peaks in 2028 under our forecast). Even with the expected 
slowdown, the impact on GDP growth will be sizable: we estimate that AI investment will 
boost GDP growth by 0.4pp globally. And although we again estimate only a small 0.2pp 
boost to measured US GDP growth in 2027 owing to continued undercounting of 
semiconductor investment and chip-related intellectual property exports, we estimate a 
larger 0.6pp boost to “true” GDP growth after correcting for these measurement biases.

### Page 5

The other notable growth impulse from 2026 that will continue in 2027 is a drag on 
rest-of-the-world growth from China. Our forecasts for China remain above consensus 
for 2027 (4.5% GS vs. 4.4% consensus), but this again reflects very robust expectations 
for Chinese exports and a further increase in China’s trade surplus (Exhibit 5). By 
contrast, we expect domestic demand growth to remain weak in 2027 owing to a 1.3pp 
of GDP property sector drag and weak underlying consumer spending, resulting in a 
modest 0.1pp headwind to GDP growth for the rest of the world, with larger growth 
drags in manufacturing-heavy economies such as Germany (-0.3pp) and Central Europe 
(-0.2pp).

### Page 6

Taken together, our 2027 global GDP growth forecast is modestly optimistic at 2.8%, a 
pace that is 0.1pp above our estimate of potential. As has been the case for the last 
several years, we are more optimistic on US growth (2.4% vs. 2.2% consensus) owing to 
resilient investment and more downbeat on the European growth outlook (0.9% vs. 
1.3%) owing to tighter financial conditions and our expectation that higher energy 
prices will start to bite this winter. We also expect continued robust growth in South 
Korea (3.2% vs. 2.7%) and Taiwan (6.3% vs. 5.9%) reflecting another year of strong AI 
exports.

Downside Risks 
We see risks around our baseline global growth forecasts as skewed to the downside for 
three reasons. 
1. A Renewed Energy Shock 
The first and most immediate risk is that an escalation of the war in Iran could lead to 
more significant energy price increases, creating growth headwinds that—unlike in 
2026—FCI and fiscal impulses would not offset in 2027. While our commodity strategists 
see risks around their energy price baselines as two-sided, they put more weight on an 
upside scenario where intensified shipping attacks, damage to oil infrastructure, and a 
more persistent risk premium push oil prices up to $132/bbl (Exhibit 7). If these risks 
materialize, the energy price impulse would remain a moderate drag on global GDP 
growth in 2027.

2. Bond Market Turbulence 
Second, we worry about a bigger fiscal risk premium. In 2026, we saw the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—show 
up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates. Although rates have eased 
somewhat over the last few weeks, the DM yield curve remains 85bp above its level in 
early 2026. If sustained, we estimate that this increase would subtract a cumulative 0.5% 
from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment (Exhibit 
8). Only part of this drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect 
bond yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.

### Page 9

3. A Weaker AI Impulse
The transition to an AI-enabled economy is occurring in two phases. The first phase
involves an investment boom that raises both corporate profits and global GDP growth
but features only limited productivity gains and labor market disruption (because it
takes firms several years to adopt AI and reorganize to take full advantage of the new
technology). The second phase involves lower hardware investment but more visible
business reorganization, as well as more substantial productivity gains and labor market

disruption.
To illustrate this transition, we overlay a simulated AI capex path with our estimates of
the labor market impacts under a 10-year adoption timeline (Exhibit 10). The capex
path assumes that US AI investment follows equity analysts’ projections through 2032,
after which the capital stock converges toward a long-run equilibrium level.1  Under our
baseline calibration, the AI capital stock eventually converges to a long-run level of
around 9% of GDP, and 9% of total US workers are displaced. (We continue to expect

most of these displaced workers to find new jobs, including in new occupations that are
created by AI).
Under these assumptions, the transition from the first phase to the second phase
doesn’t occur until 2028-2029, when we see a further pickup in the underlying US
productivity trend to the 2½-3% range and a gradual increase in the US unemployment
rate to 4½%. A backloaded transition is consistent with the projections by equity
analysts that AI investment will keep growing through 2028, albeit at a slower pace than

in 2026-2027. It is also consistent with the renewed pickup in US employment growth
since the spring, which has temporarily laid to rest concerns around imminent AI-related
job market disruption.
1 We characterize the long-run equilibrium level following Hall and Jorgenson (1967) as a function of the
depreciation rate, technological cost disinflation, nominal interest rates, and the AI capital factor share of total
output.
12 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 10

But it is hard to be sure about the timing of this shift and we see some signs that it could 
occur earlier.  Hyperscaler equity price reactions to earnings call signals for higher capex 
switched from positive in 2024-2025 to slightly negative in 2026 as the market has 
become concerned about overinvestment (Exhibit 11). Furthermore, upward revisions to 
hyperscaler capex slowed in the Q3 earnings season, which suggests that the AI 
investment cycle peak is shifting more clearly into view.

### Page 11

Beyond the fundamental demand for compute, we see three other near-term downside 
risks for AI investment: 
Capacity constraints – Semiconductor and memory producers are capacity n
constrained with order backlogs through 2028. While capacity investments currently 
underway should eventually alleviate these constraints, a surge in nominal 
investment in 2027 may increasingly raise prices (thereby pushing central banks 
toward more hawkish policy) without boosting real output. 
Credit markets – Global AI-related debt issuance has totaled $687bn so far in 2026 n
while AI-related equity issuance has added another $115bn. While our market 
strategists see adequate capital supply to meet even higher capital demand next 
year, upward pressure on borrowing costs could lead to more meaningful crowd-out 
of other investment in 2027 (vs. only modest crowd-out in 2026). 
Regulation – Concerns regarding regulatory headwinds to investment surged after n
the US midterm elections in early November raised the possibility of data center 
permitting moratoria in several states (particularly in Texas). We do not anticipate 
that regulation will derail 2027 investment, both because moratoria will likely be 
short-lived and because permits for 2027 projects have mostly already been issued. 
But a more extended delay or stringent regulatory shift would raise downside risks to 
AI investment in 2028-2029, which could weigh on growth in 2027 via tighter 
financial conditions. 
Turning to the labor market, we have argued that AI adoption is likely to displace jobs 
and cause a temporary increase in the unemployment rate of around ½ a percentage 
point over the next several years.2  Our baseline expectation is that this increase occurs 
in a backloaded fashion, with only a small effect in 2027 and more notable effects in 
2028-2029.  However, we are starting to see signs that the AI impact is spreading 
2  See “Revisiting the Extent and Timing of AI Labor Displacement,” Global Economics Analyst, October 22, 
2026.

beyond technology to the broader economy. Across nonmanufacturing industries, the 
share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment (correlations were insignificant at the 
start of the year, Exhibit 12).

Moreover, AI-driven job displacement would likely accelerate in a downside growth 
scenario. Exhibit 13 shows that the trend decline in the employment share of routine 
occupations over the past five decades has been highly concentrated in recessions.  
Given that AI has sharply increased the number of occupations exposed to automation, 
we would expect corporate leaders to respond to a slowdown by reducing headcount 
targets more aggressively, putting more upward pressure on the unemployment rate.

### Page 13

To be clear, we still expect AI-related pressure on the labor market to remain temporary.  
Employment in existing industries such as healthcare is likely to keep growing rapidly, 
and history shows that technological revolutions ultimately result in the creation of new 
industries and occupations that absorb workers displaced elsewhere.  Historically, this 
dynamism has kept the unemployment rate from increasing permanently despite all the 
labor-saving technological innovations of the past 250 years.  But over the next few 
years, job market disruption is likely to grow.  
Inflation: Progress Not Perfection 
Inflation progress was slower than expected in 2026 owing to price increases associated 
with the war in Iran.  But under our baseline commodity forecasts for a steady decline in 
energy prices next year, inflation should slow in 2027. 
The case for disinflation is particularly strong in the US because the price boosts from 
tariffs (which are still adding 40bp to year-over-year core PCE inflation through 
October) should fully drop out of the year-over-year inflation statistics next spring 
(Exhibit 14). Combined with a much smaller inflation boost from the war in Iran, we 
expect core PCE to slow from 3.2% at end-2026 to 2.4% at end-2027.

Despite our expectation for progress, we see several reasons why inflation may remain 
somewhat firm in early 2027, thereby delaying a return to target-consistent levels in 
most economies. 
First, while most of the price boost from higher energy prices has been realized, some 
pass-through will spill into next year (Exhibit 15).  This is true both in the US and 
especially in Europe, where indirect effects have so far been very limited.  For example, 
European airfares have not repriced higher in response to fuel cost increases so far—
reflecting fuel hedges, greater competition, and pre-war plans for supply 
expansion—but should pick up notably next year.

### Page 15

Second, global memory price forecasts were again revised higher over the last earnings 
season on the back of robust demand (Exhibit 16).  This pattern both suggests more 
near-term upside to core inflation from AI spillovers—although we still anticipate price 
increases should slow as production capacity increases—and points to upside risks if 
recent pricing trends continue.  This is especially true in the US, where the impact from 
higher memory prices is exaggerated because the weight of the software and 
accessories category is overstated.

Third, upstream cost pressures have increased and broadened (Exhibit 17). These 
increases are accounted for in our baseline forecasts, but historical patterns suggest that 
the recent rise in upstream cost pressures will keep inflation surprise risks elevated 
through the spring. We are particularly concerned about a reacceleration in the US, 
where the “January effect” has driven a seasonal inflation pickup almost every year since 
the pandemic (our forecast incorporates a 0.1pp boost to January core PCE).

We still see most of these price pressures as backward-looking and temporary, however, 
and remain less worried about a sustained inflation overshoot.  We take particular 
comfort in the downward trend in wage growth in most major economies that lowers the 
risk of a pickup in core services inflation (Exhibit 18).  Furthermore, leading indicators 
continue to point toward an incremental softening in US shelter inflation, and we have 
yet to see signs of second-round effects in Europe.

Taken together, we forecast a steady decline in year-over-year core inflation in the US 
and a more delayed decline in other major economies once the energy price shock is 
fully digested (Exhibit 19).  And although core inflation remains somewhat above target 
at end-2027 on a year-over-year basis, we expect that the inflation constraint will

increasingly become less binding in most economies as sequential inflation eases over 
the course of the year.

The largest risk to our near-term inflation view is that any renewed escalation in Iran 
could prolong headline (and to some degree core) inflation pressures.  Applying our 
standard rules of thumb to our commodity team’s adverse scenario implies 0.9pp upside 
to headline inflation and 0.2pp upside to core inflation relative to our baseline forecasts 
for 2027. 
Still Expecting Fewer Hikes Than Priced 
We forecast incremental policy tightening through early 2027 in most major economies 
(and see a low bar for modest incremental tightening in others), but our economic 
forecasts support a dovish view on rates in 2027: central banks could either deliver 
gradual normalization cuts later in the year as inflation comes down or pivot to cuts 
sooner if the downside growth risks discussed above materialize. 
In the US, we expect the Fed to deliver rate hikes in December and March in response to 
lingering inflation pressures (Exhibit 20).  But softer inflation data should allow the Fed 
to cut once in December 2027, followed by three more cuts in 2028 to a terminal rate of 
3.25-3.5%.  Furthermore, we see more dovish rate risks in the US than in most other 
economies, both because US inflation drivers look more clearly temporary and because 
the US is most exposed to a downturn in the AI cycle.

### Page 18

Our views on other major DMs also skew dovish (Exhibit 21). We expect the ECB to 
deliver its final hike in December, before starting to normalize back toward a 2.25% 
terminal rate in 2027Q4. And although higher-than-expected inflation could prompt 
another hike in Q1, our softer European growth outlook limits policy rate upside. In the 
UK, we expect a final BoE hike to 4.25% in February before a hold for the remainder of 
2027. We likewise expect normalization cuts from the BoE to a 3.25% terminal rate, but 
not until 2028. Our ECB and BoE forecasts are dovish relative to market pricing on both a 
baseline and a risk-adjusted basis. 
We are also dovish on Canada and Australia. In Canada, we expect two hikes to 3.0% in 
the first half of next year based on recent BoC communication suggesting it plans to 
return to the center of its neutral range (which we believe will be revised up to 2.5-3.5% 
in April).  But with trade uncertainty looming large, it is more likely that the BoC 
underdelivers than hikes above the midpoint of its neutral range (as the market is 
pricing).  In Australia, our RBA forecast for a hold at 4.85% through October is broadly in 
line with market pricing over 2027H1, but more dovish in 2027H2 and especially by 
end-2028 (by which time we expect a cumulative 125bp in cuts to have been delivered).  
The main outlier to our forecast of only modest further policy tightening is Japan. We 
expect the BoJ to deliver another 25bp hike at the December MPM, reflecting recent 
BoJ communication highlighting both upside inflation risks and the impact of higher 
global interest rates on the Yen. But with core CPI (ex fresh food but including energy) 
likely to rise above 3.5% through next summer, we now expect that the BoJ will follow 
through with additional hikes in March and June for a 2.0% terminal rate.

Our views also skew dovish in EMs (Exhibit 22). This is particularly true in CEEMEA and 
LatAm, where we expect most central banks to cut through 2027.  In Asia, we expect 
rates to stay flat or rise slightly (though our forecasts are still below market pricing 
except in Taiwan). Energy prices are the main upside risk to EM rates, but we think 
markets overestimate the inflation risk in most EMs for three reasons. First, inflation was 
already low before the energy shock. Second, EM economies are cyclically weaker than 
DMs. Third, EM currencies have held up well, supported by healthy external balances. 
Some EMs may tighten further over the next few quarters, but we expect most to hold or 
cut after that.

### Page 20

More Challenges for Markets than the Macro
As with the economic outlook, the market outlook continues to be dominated by the
path of the AI cycle and energy risks related to the Iran war.
Our baseline forecast is quite friendly. The mix of steady growth, falling core inflation,
gradually moderating energy prices and an earlier peak in G10 hiking cycles than market
pricing does not signal obvious threats to asset markets. The clearest difference with

market pricing is our more benign view of the policy rate path for many economies,
which all else equal should prove a boost to both equities and bonds. Our central asset
forecasts look for positive returns across the major asset classes. After the recent
sell-off, our forecasts imply robust equity returns between now and the end of 2027, but
we expect the pace of gains to slow as we move through next year.
Despite this baseline, we think the market outlook will continue to prove challenging, as

it has been already for several months. In part, this is because of the risks around our
baseline that we have already described. In part, it is because we think markets—
especially around the AI theme—have been priced in ways that leave them more
vulnerable to signs that important tailwinds may be starting to fade. In the process, the
volatility and rotations that have mostly remained under the surface of major equity and
credit markets this year could spill over more into shifts at the index level.

Managing the Peak in the AI Investment Cycle
The biggest uncertainties—as with the economic outlook—lie around the AI cycle. With
AI investment growth expected to remain robust, the peak in the AI investment impulse
should be a manageable event for the economy. But it is already proving to be a bigger
test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger
contributor to growth in earnings, market value and capital spending than to growth in

GDP. Second, AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity
gains, especially companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have
made them more vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the
point at which growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters,
even if underlying growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through
that peak allows markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and as

growth decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as
we may be starting to see for the AI capex cycle.
12 November 2026   20
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 21

Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant derating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are most 
sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. The good news is that robust profits, and a rising 
share of income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and 
the deep valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this 
cycle is whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in 
profits, which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove 
persistent especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows 
from the EM crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation 
allowed—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure on 
the cost of capital.

Exhibit 23: AI Impulse Has Been a Much Larger Contributor to Growth in US Earnings and 
Market Value than to Growth in US GDP
